Hello, doctor. Thank you for taking the time to see me today. I, um... I hope I'm explaining this right. 

I guess I came in because I just can't seem to shake this... this heavy feeling that I've completely failed. As a father, mostly, and just as a useful person in general. Well, um... I usually have a few things I really enjoy. I've always loved working on my model railway, and watching the Cubs games. My daughter and I used to really look forward to those games together.

It's just that it keeps looping in my head, over and over, until I start feeling like I don't really deserve much of a future. \n\nI try to push those thoughts away, I really do, but they've been pretty stubborn lately. Does that make sense? Is that the sort of thing you needed to know?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these feelings of failure?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess it's been about a month now. It started right around the time my daughter left for college out of state. \n\nI mean, it's completely normal to miss your kids when they leave home, right? Every parent goes through it. I probably shouldn't even be making such a big deal out of it... I'm sure you see people with much worse problems than empty nest syndrome. But, yes, it's been about a month. I hope that's the kind of timeline you were looking for?\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying activities or hobbies that you normally find pleasurable over the past month?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I usually have a few things I really enjoy. I've always loved working on my model railway, and watching the Cubs games. My daughter and I used to really look forward to those games together. \n\nBut... I guess lately I haven't been keeping up with them as much as I probably should. The trains are just sort of sitting half-built on the table right now. I keep telling myself I need to finish them, I really do. And even just turning on a game on the TV... I don't know, it just feels a bit pointless lately. \n\nI know I shouldn't let myself get so lazy about the things I enjoy, and I'm sure I'll snap out of this little slump soon. I'm sorry, I know that's not a very good excuse. Does that answer your question? It's not heavy lifting or anything. But when I look at them, I just... I don't feel that spark I used to get. And the Cubs games... even just pressing the button on the remote feels like too much effort for something that doesn't bring me any joy right now. \n\nI feel terrible admitting that, it sounds so ungrateful to not even enjoy a simple baseball game. I'm sure it's just a phase and my interest will come back. I hope I'm explaining the difference clearly enough? The trains are just sort of sitting half-built on the table right now. I keep telling myself I need to finish them, I really do. And even just turning on a game on the TV... I don't know, it just feels a bit pointless lately. \n\nI know I shouldn't let myself get so lazy about the things I enjoy, and I'm sure I'll snap out of this little slump soon. I'm sorry, I know that's not a very good excuse. Does that answer your question? I hope I'm giving you what you need to know.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling less interested in things you usually enjoy, beyond just not having the energy to do them?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess so, yes. It's not just that I'm too tired to work on the trains or watch the games. It's more like... the feeling of looking forward to them is just gone. \n\nLike I said, the trains are just sitting there half-built, and I know I have the physical energy to sit at the table and glue some pieces together. It's not heavy lifting or anything. But when I look at them, I just... I don't feel that spark I used to get. And the Cubs games... even just pressing the button on the remote feels like too much effort for something that doesn't bring me any joy right now. \n\nI feel terrible admitting that, it sounds so ungrateful to not even enjoy a simple baseball game. I'm sure it's just a phase and my interest will come back. I hope I'm explaining the difference clearly enough? I want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of information.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts about harming yourself or wishing you were dead?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really don't want to alarm you, and I promise I wouldn't ever actually do anything to hurt myself. I know how awful that would be for my daughter. But... if I'm being completely honest, because I know you need the truth to do your job... I guess I have had a few passing thoughts lately that it would just be easier if I didn't wake up in the morning. \n\nIt's probably just the stress talking, I'm sure. I sort of caught myself going over all my finances and paperwork, almost like I was... well, getting things ready for someone else to take over. \n\nI know I shouldn't let my mind go to those dark places, and I'm really sorry if that sounds terrible. I don't want you to think I'm crazy or anything. Is it... is it somewhat normal to have thoughts like that when you're in a slump like this? Um... definitely nothing like that, I promise. \n\nI know I mentioned being a bit foggy and having trouble with my focus earlier, but my mind is still very much my own. I promise you that. I know how terrible that would be for my family, especially my daughter. I'm not that kind of person, I really aren't. \n\nIt's just... well, like I said, sometimes my mind just wanders to this idea that it would be so much easier if I just... slipped away in my sleep. Just didn't wake up. It's just a fleeting thought, really! But... I guess I did do something a little silly the other day that sort of spooked me. \n\nI was looking over all my old financial spreadsheets, making sure everything was perfectly organized and clearly labeled... and I realized I was doing it so that it would be easy for someone else to take over if I wasn't around. I'm not making any actual plans or anything like that, I swear. I'm safe, I promise. Is it okay that I told you that? ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, um... no. No, never anything like this. I promise you, I've always been a very, um, steady person. Very reliable. 

I mean, everyone has their off days or feels a little stressed sometimes, right? But I've never had these kinds of... well, these dark thoughts, or this much trouble just getting through a normal day. I've always been very capable. I got my degree, I always took care of my responsibilities... I've never been the type of guy to just let things slide or fall apart. 

So this is all completely new for me. I really hope you don't think I've been hiding a long history of this from you or anything like that. I'm usually very put-together, I swear. I'm sorry if that doesn't give you much background to work with... is it okay that this is the first time it's happened? I just really want to make sure I'm giving you the answers you need to figure this out.

Well, um... I guess if I'm being completely honest, yes. It's been most of the day, pretty much every day. But really, it's only been going on for about a month! It's not like I've been like this forever.

Oh, um... no. Like I think I mentioned just a little bit ago, I really haven't. I've never had a time in my life where I felt like this before. 

I've always been a pretty driven person, you know? I always took a lot of pride in what I did, and I certainly never felt like a... well, like a failure, until recently. And I always really enjoyed my hobbies and keeping active. So, no, there weren't any past times like this at all.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, um... I guess I'd have to say yes, it is starting to interfere a bit. I mean, I try really hard to keep on top of things, I really do. But... well, my bills and some of the house projects I started are kind of piling up on me. I just... I sit down to do them and I can't seem to make myself follow through. I always used to be so organized, so it's a little embarrassing to admit that things are slipping. 

And, um, socially... I guess I've pulled back a little. I used to go to a book club, and I'd go on bike rides with a few guys I know, but I've sort of stopped seeing them lately. I just haven't had the energy to keep up appearances, I suppose. I hope that doesn't make me sound like a bad friend. I'm sure they understand I'm just going through a phase.

But, um... I guess the part that really made me realize I couldn't just brush this off anymore is... well, I started organizing my finances. But, like, organizing them in a way... like someone else might need to take over them. I don't know why I did that, it just felt like something I should do. 

I'm sorry, I know that sounds a bit alarming. I promise I'm not trying to be dramatic. I just want to make sure I'm giving you the full picture so you can help me. Is it okay that I shared that? I just want to make sure I'm doing this right.
